### Machina Nova

| name | polhem\_machina\_nova |
| --- | --- |
| display\_name | Machina Nova |
| description | Christopher Polhem, dubbed the 'father of Swedish engineering, has transformed Swedish industry with his *Machina Nova*, an innovative array of mechanical systems designed to streamline and boost production. Using intricate systems of gears, cams, and water power, Polhem has developed several machines capable of performing tasks previously done by hand, from ore-crushing in mines to textile weaving and sawmilling. |

### Madurese Ghali

| name | maj\_the\_ghali |
| --- | --- |
| display\_name | Madurese Ghali |
| description | The people that populate the Madura Strait have come to serve in our navy. Their naval prowess and great shipbuilding skills have given rise to a new kind of vessel, the Madurese Ghali. ### Maghrebi Administration

| name | maghrebi\_administration |
| --- | --- |
| display\_name | Maghrebi Administration |

### Magna Carta

| name | magna\_carta\_advance |
| --- | --- |
| display\_name | Magna Carta |
| description | The 'Great Charter' is a legal document that aims to protect the feudal rights of our barons against unjust taxation and legal maneuvers by the Crown. ### Mahābhārata

| name | mahabarata |
| --- | --- |
| display\_name | Mahābhārata |
| description | One of the two major Smṛti texts together with the Rāmāyaṇa, the Mahābhārata is an epic of ancient India. It is the longest epic poem ever written, about ten times bigger than the Greek *Iliad* and *Odyssey* combined, and it narrates the story of a dynastic struggle for the throne of the ancient Indian kingdom of Kuru culminating in the Kurukshetra War, also called Mahābhārata War. The story ends with the death of Kṛṣṇa and the end of his dynasty. |

### Mainzer Königreich

| name | mai\_mainz\_kingdom |
| --- | --- |
| display\_name | Mainzer Königreich |
| description | While celebrations on Ash Wednesday are a common occurrence, they hold especially deep roots in our country. While the public celebrates the *Fastnacht*, or carnival, with masquerade and excessive feasts, our court will engage in a special event. During the carnival celebrations, we will redistribute the roles of our court per lottery, with even the ruler having to participate. This custom of swapping roles, called *Mainzer Königreich*, will spread far beyond our borders and help expand our cultural legacy. |

### Makeshift Repairs

| name | rudimentary\_coastal\_ship\_repair |
| --- | --- |
| display\_name | Makeshift Repairs |
| description | It is unavoidable that ships deteriorate while sailing. The beating of the waves and the bite of saltwater accumulate over time, damaging both the hull and ropes no matter how well-constructed the vessel. To alleviate this, sailors are used to performing regular maintenance on their ships to keep them in prime condition, and when more significant damage is received, even being able to keep it afloat until they reach a safe harbor. |

| description | The Manchu alphabet has emerged, combining Classical Mongolian characters with Jurchen phonetics. This unique writing system facilitates left-to-right vertical column writing. ### Maritime Fiefdoms of the Adriatic

| name | zta\_maritime\_fiefdoms\_of\_the\_adriatic |
| --- | --- |
| display\_name | Maritime Fiefdoms of the Adriatic |
| description | Our identity is forged at the violent meeting point of the high limestone mountain and the salt of the sea. By maintaining firm control over the strategic coastal towns of the coast, the lords of Zeta act as the indispensable maritime gateway for the entire Serbian interior, controlling the flow of salt, silk, and silver. We utilize the considerable wealth generated from Mediterranean trade and customs duties to fund our independent-minded mountain clans and build massive stone fortifications that guard the shoreline. This ensures that Zeta remains a maritime power in its own right, projecting influence far beyond the rugged cliffs of the hinterland. |

### Maritime Republic Legacy

| name | pis\_maritimes\_legacy |
| --- | --- |
| display\_name | Maritime Republic Legacy |
| description | For three centuries, Pisa stood among the dominant sea powers of the western Mediterranean, its galleys reaching every harbor from Sardinia to the Levant and its naval victories over the Saracens earning the city privileged access to markets that lesser powers could not enter. The defeat at Meloria in 1284 diminished our fleet, but could not erase what those centuries of seaborne commerce built — our captains know these waters, our traders know their counterparts in every port, and the routes that made us great are still routes that we can sail. This seafaring legacy gives our merchants an advantage in the Western trade sphere that no landlocked rival can replicate, and the memory of maritime greatness drives us to rebuild what was taken from us. |

### Masters of Silk

| name | lombard\_silk\_masters |
| --- | --- |
| display\_name | Masters of Silk |
| description | The mulberry groves and silk workshops of Lake Como and the Lombard plain supply the courts of Europe with the finest silk cloth available, and our weavers have refined the production process to a degree that foreign competitors struggle to replicate. We protect this advantage by regulating the export of raw silk and the techniques of our master weavers, ensuring that the value-added work stays within our borders rather than leaving as cheap raw material. This control over the full production chain turns silk from a local trade into a steady source of revenue that holds its value even when other commodity prices fall. |

| --- | --- |
| display\_name | Masters of the Alpine Passes |
| description | The great mountains of the Alps are not mere obstacles to us, they are our home. Their great passes — the Gotthard, the Simplon, the Grimsel — are our streets. ### Mediterranean Cosmopolitanism

| name | dalmatian\_mediterranean\_cosmopolitanism |
| --- | --- |
| display\_name | Mediterranean Cosmopolitanism |
| description | Centuries of maritime trade and bilingualism have produced a culture of Cosmopolitanism, where our people are at home in any port from Venice to the Levant. This cultural openness allows us to absorb and adapt the best ideas from across the globe, creating a society that is uniquely flexible and resilient. Our people are the 'citizens of the sea', possessing a world-view that is far broader than that of the landlocked interior. This cosmopolitan spirit ensures that the Dalmatian culture remains vibrant and commercially successful, serving as the 'window to the world' for the entire Balkan Peninsula. |

### Mediterranean Galley

| name | unlock\_m\_galley\_advance |
| --- | --- |
| display\_name | Mediterranean Galley |
| description | With roots back to the ancient ships of antiquity, the galley remained the prime choice for Mediterranean naval warfare until the 16th century. The galley was primarily propelled by oars, usually about 25 pairs manned by up to three men each. This meant that they were less dependent on wind gauges compared to sailing ships, and their maneuverability made them a feared adversary. Eventually, the galley went out of regular use with the introduction of more advanced oceangoing men-of-war, but remained useful in shallow waters until the 18th century. |
| icon | Navy galley.png |

### Meteora Sanctuaries

| name | ths\_meteora\_sanctuaries\_advance |
| --- | --- |
| display\_name | Meteora Sanctuaries |
| description | As the Balkans face increasing instability, the Despots of Thessaly patronize the construction of monastic complexes atop the inaccessible sandstone pillars of Meteora. These 'monasteries in the sky' serve a dual purpose: They function as spiritual beacons for the Orthodox faith and as high-altitude treasuries where the state's wealth and holy relics can be protected from marauding mercenaries. By centralizing the spiritual authority of the region in such defensible positions, the Despotate ensures that the local identity remains anchored in Orthodoxy even when the central government is under duress. |

| description | Refining centuries of mountain warfare, we have implemented the 'National Redoubt' doctrine, where every major city and pass is integrated into a single, cohesive defensive plan. ### Mongolian Mobility

| name | mge\_mongolian\_mobility |
| --- | --- |
| display\_name | Mongolian Mobility |
| description | Each of our soldiers typically maintains three or four horses, allowing them to change each time their mount becomes tired, and thereby travel at high speed for days without stopping. Furthermore, their extra mounts provide them with food reserves through mare's milk as well as horse meat, making our armies far less vulnerable to starvation than other, so-called civilized nations' armies. This allows us to outmaneuver our enemies with ease, covering a staggering amount of territory in shorter periods than they had believed to be possible. |

### Monte dei Paschi

| name | sie\_monte\_dei\_paschi |
| --- | --- |
| display\_name | Monte dei Paschi |
| description | Our magistrates have established the *Monte dei Paschi* — a public lending institution secured against the revenues of the Maremma pastures — to provide affordable credit to merchants and the poor alike and to give Siena a stable foundation for commerce in an era of expanding trade. This innovation in public finance became a model of banking stability that outlasted empires, sustaining our commercial life through crises that bankrupted more reckless rivals. This institution accelerates the adoption of new economic practices across our lands while generating the steady fiscal revenue that a city of ambition requires. |

### Monteverdi's L'Orfeo

| name | man\_monteverdi\_orfeo |
| --- | --- |
| display\_name | Monteverdi's L'Orfeo |
| description | When Claudio Monteverdi staged *L'Orfeo* in our Palazzo Ducale for the Accademia degli Invaghiti, he gave the world its first fully realized opera — a work in which voices, instruments, and drama were fused into a form that audiences immediately recognized as unlike anything they had heard before. Mantua was the city where this transformation happened, and its court was the institution that made it possible, providing Monteverdi with singers, players, and the sustained patronage without which neither the rehearsals nor the score could have materialized. This primacy in the birth of opera makes our court the acknowledged origin of Europe's most powerful new art form, and the artists trained in its shadow carry our reputation to every stage they subsequently inhabit. |
| icon | Advance man monteverdi orfeo.png |

### Morean Silk

| name | ach\_morean\_silk\_advance |
| --- | --- |
| display\_name | Morean Silk |
| description | The Peloponnese remains the heart of European silk production, a legacy of the Byzantine era that the Frankish Princes have expanded through state-sponsored mulberry groves. By controlling strategic ports, we can undercut the high prices of Persian and Chinese imports, making Achaea the primary supplier for the garment makers of Florence and Lyon. This monopoly provides a steady stream of *Soft Gold* that funds our mercenaries and the fortification of our mountain passes. |

### Morlach Frontier Levies

| name | dal\_morlach\_frontier\_levies |
| --- | --- |
| display\_name | Morlach Frontier Levies |
| description | We have integrated the Morlachs — the fierce Vlach and Slavic shepherds of the interior — into a permanent military reserve. These irregulars are granted land rights in exchange for constant readiness, serving as the eyes and ears of the nation. Their intimate knowledge of the karst geography allows us to conduct deep-strike ambushes and night raids that paralyze the enemy's logistics. This creates a 'Nation in Arms' culture in the Dalmatian hinterland, where the boundary between civilian and soldier is entirely erased in the name of our defense. |

### Municipal Liberties

| name | milanese\_tradition |
| --- | --- |
| display\_name | Municipal Liberties |
| description | The cities of Lombardy have governed themselves through elected consuls and independent courts since the time of the Lombard League, building a tradition of civic law that no emperor has managed to fully dismantle. We draw on this legacy by codifying these municipal liberties into a formal charter, granting our towns a recognized degree of self-governance in exchange for their loyal contribution of taxes and troops. This compact between the crown and the city keeps our urban population engaged and invested, reducing the friction that bleeds other states dry whenever they need to call on their subjects. |
| icon | Advance milanese tradition.png |

### Mura Malatestiani

| name | romagnol\_mura\_malatestiani |
| --- | --- |
| display\_name | Mura Malatestiani |
| description | Romagnol towns, caught between the Papal States and Venice, have become laboratories for defensive architecture. The region has pioneered the use of the *Roca* (*Rocca* in standard Italian: the Fortress), a fortress with rounded towers that deflect fire from primitive cannons. These fortifications mean that every village is a tactical strongpoint. They will force invaders into a slow, attritional style of warfare, allowing the small Romagnol states to punch far above their weight in Mediterranean power politics. |
